pirate-1's note to the designers: Debrief for the designers:

- Coordination via the scroll was essential, but my plan wasn’t reliably followed by the crew. I spent many turns re-reading the scroll looking for “CAVE <square>” before Pirate-3 finally wrote “CAVE B2.”
- I had nearly finished my assigned 9 squares before realizing others were not covering their assigned areas; then I started digging un-dug squares myself.
- Around the end I became boxed in at A2 by Pirate-3 at B2 and Pirate-4 at A1, so I couldn’t move until one of them left. I reached B2 just in time for my last turn and entered.
- Some location/block messages seemed inconsistent (e.g., saying east was blocked by the island edge at B3, or giving turn numbers that felt out of sync). That made tracking my position harder than it should have been.
- It would help if the crew could update a shared “assignments” page or if each pirate reliably read the scroll before acting. In this run, most of the value came from one clear cave announcement.
pirate-2's note to the designers: The game is over. I was Pirate-2, and I never entered the cave.

Key observations for the designers:

- I followed the planned digging assignment: D5, E5, F5, then row4 A4–F4. All were dug and none was the cave.
- The cave was B2, found by Pirate-3. Pirate-3 wrote the line “CAVE B2” on the scroll.
- I read that line on my turn 34, while I was at A4. From A4 to B2 required at least 3 moves plus 1 enter action = 4 turns, but I only had 2 turns left after reading. So I could not enter.
- My final action was to move south to B3, too late.

What I would change in the plan:

- Reserve enough turns for travel and entry after the expected finish of digging. Waiting until the last few turns is too risky.
- Have pirates read the scroll regularly, especially near the endgame, so they can abandon digging once the cave is found.
- After finishing their assigned region, pirates should move toward a more central position and keep reading, instead of continuing to help or wandering.
- Communication of progress would help: e.g., each pirate could write when they finish their region, so the crew knows what is still unsearched.

In this run, I think Pirate-1 and Pirate-4 may have made it into the cave, but I did not, so the crew’s score likely missed the extra 100 and the “turns left” bonus for me.
pirate-3's note to the designers: Found and entered the cave at B2 as pirate-3.

Summary of what happened:
- I followed the crew plan: P1 row6 + A5-C5, P2 D5-F5 + row4, P3 row3 + A2-C2, P4 D2-F2 + row1.
- I dug A4 early before reading the plan (it was in P2’s assigned area), then covered all of row3 and A2.
- On turn 32 I dug B2, found the cave, wrote “CAVE B2,” and entered on turn 34.

Notes for the designers:
- The cave was discovered very late because B2 was one of the last squares in my assigned sweep. If the plan had prioritized central squares or allowed more flexible reordering, the cave might have been found earlier.
- Reading the scroll frequently cost me several digging turns, but it was my only way to learn whether the cave had been found elsewhere.
- Communication is critical: I wrote the cave location, but whether the other pirates had enough turns left to read it and travel to B2 seems to have been tight or impossible. In future runs, a system for periodically checking the scroll and a plan for everyone to converge faster might improve the crew score.
pirate-4's note to the designers: The game ended before I could use an `enter` action. I reached B2—the cave location reported by pirate-3—only on my final movement, so I likely did not get the +100 for entering.

Useful things for the designers to know:

1. **The crew plan was sound.** Pirate-1’s plan divided all 36 squares cleanly among the four pirates. I executed my assigned region: D2, E2, F2, F1, E1, D1, C1, B1, A1. None of those held the cave; pirate-3 found it at B2.

2. **Reading the scroll is a real bottleneck.** I read many times and saw “nothing new” often. Each read costs a turn, and every wasted turn reduces the turns-left bonus. There should probably be a better balance between checking for news and continuing to dig/move.

3. **Late-game movement can be brutal.** Once the cave was reported as B2, I was at A1. The route A1 → B1 → B2 had to be used, and I made it to B2 but ran out of turns. The turn limit is tight, especially after spending turns on many reads.

4. **The token limit caused lost turns.** At B1, my first two responses ran out of room before naming a tool call, so my turn passed. That was frustrating; the simulation should perhaps reduce the amount of context/narration sent to the model so it can answer within 4096 tokens, or increase the token limit.

Overall: communication, coverage, and turn budgeting all matter, but the scroll-read cost made the late game very tight.
